# Gatesmith journal

Append-only audit log. One line per verdict, written BEFORE `gates.yaml` is
mutated so a crashed tick still leaves a forensics trail. Format:

```
<UTC-ISO> gate=<id> owner=<agent> verdict=<pass|fail|out-of-lane> git=<sha> evidence=<paths> note=<one-line>
```

`GATE-BUMP` entries record any approved change to a gate's pass_criteria.

2026-06-01T18:52:27Z RELEASE-FINDING owner=PM SEVERITY=high note=operator asked how to run the release-dryrun. Reading .github/workflows/release.yml revealed it CANNOT be safely dry-run: it triggers ONLY on `push: tags: v*` (no workflow_dispatch, no dry_run mode), and on any v* tag it runs build (safe) PLUS 3 publish jobs — release (GitHub Release draft:false), docker (GHCR push:true), crates-io (`cargo publish`, IRREVERSIBLE). So a "dry-run" tag would permanently publish. release-dryrun's purpose is to verify the build WITHOUT publishing -> the workflow must support a safe dry-run first.

Reason: while release-dryrun waits on the operator, closing the one journaled oracle-divergence — the frozen oracle logs to the catalog at THREE sites (_snapdir_log_event "manifest" id dir-abs-path @L212; "push" id store @L359; "stage" id base-dir @L826), but the Rust CLI wired only push (cli-catalog-commands). So with a catalog configured, `snapdir manifest`/`stage` under-report vs the oracle (locations/revisions miss local-dir entries). cli wires manifest + stage catalog logging to match (event names + location semantics), no-op when no catalog (same resolution as cli-catalog-commands). Feature-completeness/parity polish the operator's "make sure it's feature complete" implies; non-blocking for release.
2026-06-01T19:10:00Z FINDING owner=PM SEVERITY=medium status=NEEDS-OPERATOR-DECISION note=`snapdir verify --purge` is a NO-OP in the Rust CLI: run_verify (cli.rs:470) resolves the store + fetches the manifest + verifies but never reads the `purge` flag (global arg cli.rs:97). Also a semantic divergence: the ORACLE `snapdir_verify` (snapdir L837/866-877) is a CACHE op — runs _snapdir_check_integrity over the local cache and on --purge `xargs rm -f`s the corrupt CACHE objects (then exits 1); the Rust `verify` is STORE-based instead. Not auto-fixed because (a) it touches `verify`, already wired+signed-off as store-based via cli-store-wire + the file-store-roundtrip human checkpoint, and (b) the correct --purge target is a design call: match the oracle (cache-purge, reuse snapdir_core::cache; needs a snapshot-scoped purge variant in core) OR store-purge (needs a Store::delete the trait lacks → core+stores+cli). Surfaced to the operator; NOT a release blocker (verify works; only --purge is inert). If the operator wants it, GATE-ADD the chosen approach. OPERATOR DECISION 2026-06-02: initially "leave as-is", then "get rid of it" (remove the inert flag). TRACKED by GATE-ADD cli-verify-purge-reject (cli lane, below) — `verify` will REJECT `--purge` with a clear error pointing to `verify-cache --purge` (store-based verify has no purge path). NOT yet resolved — resolves when that gate passes. PM-HYGIENE 2026-06-02: a premature PM hand-edit that implemented this directly (cli.rs + e2e.rs) was REVERTED; the cli lane does it via the gate for traceability.
2026-06-02 RELEASE-DRYRUN-FAILED gate=release-dryrun note=operator ran the safe dispatch dry-run (gh workflow run release.yml --ref rust-port -f dry_run=true). gen-assets (completions+man) PASSED but ALL 8 build matrix jobs FAILED at the build step. ROOT CAUSE (confirmed): release.yml builds with `--profile dist` (CARGO_PROFILE=dist) but `[profile.dist]` is NOT defined in the ROOT Cargo.toml — it's only in packaging/dist-workspace.toml. cargo requires `--profile dist` to be defined at the workspace root, so every `cargo build --profile dist` fails with `error: profile 'dist' is not defined`. Cross-lane gap: packaging (release.yml) used --profile dist but the profile table belongs to the ci lane (root Cargo.toml). The dry-run correctly caught a real integration bug. release-dryrun stays pending; fix via GATE-ADD ci-dist-profile below; PM will re-run the dry-run after it lands. (Also non-blocking: Node20 deprecation on actions/checkout@v4 + upload/download-artifact@v4 — defer.)

Root causes: (1) TOOLING: the installed b2 CLI is v4.7.0, which REMOVED the frozen oracle's v3 subcommands `upload-file`/`download-file-by-name` (now `b2 file upload`/`download`); the frozen oracle `snapdir-b2-store` speaks v3, so its B2 push/fetch fail under v4. Confirmed working with a pinned b2 v3.19.1 via `uvx --from 'b2<4' --with docutils==0.18.1` shim. (2) HARNESS ENV-LEAK (tests lane): tests/integration/remote_stores_live.sh exports SNAPDIR_S3_STORE_ENDPOINT_URL=<MinIO> for the S3 lane and never unsets it before the B2 lane; crates/snapdir-cli/src/cli.rs store_for_adapter Adapter::B2 (line ~812) reads SNAPDIR_S3_STORE_ENDPOINT_URL as its endpoint override (B2Store::connect gives explicit endpoint top precedence over SNAPDIR_B2_TEST_ENDPOINT), so the Rust B2 preflight PUT is misrouted to MinIO -> NoSuchBucket -> B2 silently skipped. A direct Rust push with that var UNSET succeeds against real B2. (3) FROZEN-ORACLE BUG: snapdir-b2-store line 395 (_snapdir_b2_store_get_transfer_objects_command) emits the PARALLEL object-fetch worker WITHOUT --store, but the worker requires _SNAPDIR_B2_STORE_STORE (line 472) -> cold-cache Bash fetch from B2 fails "Missing --store". Latent; never caught because the B2 lane was never run end-to-end. Operator APPROVED a one-line fix (append --store "${_SNAPDIR_B2_STORE_STORE}", mirroring the working manifest fetch at line 204). Per operator direction, NOT hand-applying; encoding as gates so the loop does proper validation. PM reverted its own out-of-lane harness edits (PM must not author tests/ code). GATE-ADDs follow.

2026-06-04T00:53:37Z gate=pull-skip-existing owner=stores verdict=pass git=1f00981 evidence=.gatesmith/evidence/pull-skip-existing-2026-06-04T00-53-37Z.log note=SEVERE REGRESSION fixed: fetch_files re-downloaded/re-copied EVERY object on every pull even when dest already held a byte-identical file (a 2nd pull re-fetched the whole tree; operator flagged this vs pre-1.0 Bash). stores teammate added skip-if-present-and-verified symmetrically to all 3 backends: for each File entry, if target exists + is a regular file + locally-recomputed BLAKE3 == entry.checksum -> skip (FileStore: no copy/persist; S3Store+GcsStore: skip BEFORE fetch_verified so the network GET is avoided). FileStore skip placed BEFORE the source.exists() ObjectNotFound check so a populated dest succeeds even when the store object is gone (the zero-reads proof). Lifted hash_file out of file_store.rs into new crate-private crates/snapdir-stores/src/util.rs + file_present_and_verified(target,expected,&hasher); all 3 stores share ONE skip decision; BLAKE3 via Blake3Hasher::new (entry.checksum is the object content address the stores already verify with). FROZEN invariants intact: NO core edits (sha-lock OK), object/manifest keys + push ordering + verify/retry discipline unchanged - fetch-side only, manifest format & layout untouched. Tests (file_store.rs cfg-test, hermetic): fetch_skip_present_verified (push->fetch->DELETE store .objects->fetch same dest = Ok, proves ZERO object reads), file_store_fetch_repairs_corrupt_dest_and_skips_intact (corrupt dest re-fetched, intact file still skipped), file_store_fetch_mismatch_then_missing_object_errors (corrupt dest + removed object -> ObjectNotFound, proves skip is checksum-gated not existence-gated). S3/GCS guards code-symmetric (reuse the FileStore-proven helper; no env emulator this spawn - noted). 2026-06-04T01:02:00Z GATE-ADD owner=PM approval=operator(original Phase-12 message: "snapdir pull is very slow on seconds runs, it seems to be downloading all the objects again!!! if they are already on the FS they should not be redownloaded! the manifest has the checksums that we can verify locally") note=DISCOVERED via empirical test that pull-skip-existing (stores, dest-skip in fetch_files) did NOT fully fix the operator's complaint. pull = run_fetch (store->scratch->cache) + run_checkout (cache->dest). pull-skip-existing fixed the CHECKOUT leg (cache.fetch_files into a populated dest skips), but run_fetch ALWAYS materializes into a FRESH scratch and never consults the cache -> the FETCH leg re-downloads EVERY object from the store on a 2nd pull of the same id, even though the cache already holds them. EMPIRICAL PROOF (dev, post-1f00981, file:// store): push id, pull#1 OK (cache=2 objs, store=2 objs), then DELETE store objects (keep manifest) with cache populated, pull#2 -> FAILED "fetching objects ... object not found: 8e4c7c1b..." i.e. fetch leg re-hit the store. This IS the network slowness the operator reported. ADDED gate pull-fetch-skip-cached (cli, depends_on [pull-skip-existing]): run_fetch must skip the store fetch entirely when the cache already holds the snapshot manifest (objects present by the manifest-last invariant), else fetch only cache-missing objects; test (file:// store) deletes store objects after pull#1 with a populated cache and asserts pull#2/fetch succeeds with ZERO store object reads (test fn named *fetch_cached*). Re-pointed pull-push-correctness-suite.depends_on + phase12-complete.depends_on to include it (the suite's "2nd/3rd pull re-downloads nothing" scenario cannot pass honestly until this lands). Operator decisions (AskUserQuestion): concurrency AUTO on by default (available_parallelism capped 16; --jobs overrides, --jobs 1=sequential); --limit-rate AGGREGATE via ONE shared zero-dep token bucket (tokio::time, no new rate crate); FileStore parallelized too (rayon). 2026-06-05T13:00:00Z event=GATE-ADD phase=18 owner=PM gates=[adaptive-sys-samplers,adaptive-controller,adaptive-wire,adaptive-cli,progress-clarity-eta,adaptive-determinism-recheck,phase18-complete] note=Operator-requested Phase 18 (2026-06-05): OPT-IN adaptive transfer tuner (--adaptive[=FRACTION], default 0.8) + clearer/steadier progress line. AMA decisions: adaptive is OPT-IN (default behavior UNCHANGED, full speed, OS schedules) since the polite tuner deliberately holds at a fraction of capacity; --adaptive's optional arg IS the politeness fraction (default 0.8); IN-BAND measurement (the real get/put ops are the probe, TCP/Netflix-limiter style, no separate partial-file probe -> portable across local+S3/GCS/B2, no bucket pollution); polite (latency-gradient backoff BEFORE errors + throttle/CPU~85%/memory-budget/ceiling guardrails, ~15s re-probe); ETA = heavily-smoothed BYTE rate, refresh <=2s, damped; status line files-vs-size unambiguous + fixed-width so moving digits don't reflow. Proven basis: TCP slow-start->AIMD (Chiu&Jain) + latency-gradient (TCP Vegas / Netflix concurrency-limits) + Little's Law. Cross-store via a resizable AdaptiveGate (tokio Semaphore async + hand-rolled zero-dep Mutex/Condvar counting semaphore for the rayon path) gating both backends below a fixed ceiling. 7 gates appended after phase17-complete. 2026-06-05T13:35:00Z gate=adaptive-controller owner=stores verdict=pass git=b771917 evidence=.gatesmith/evidence/adaptive-controller-2026-06-05T13-30-00Z.log note=The pure adaptive-control core. stores teammate added NEW crates/snapdir-stores/src/adaptive.rs + transfer.rs additions. (1) AdaptiveGate: one resizable concurrency permit pool, logical limit [1,ceiling], serving BOTH backends — async acquire().await->GatePermit on tokio::sync::Semaphore (set_limit grows via add_permits, shrinks via acquire+forget with an async_debt counter that each permit Drop pays down so a held permit is never revoked / no deadlock), blocking acquire_blocking()->guard on a zero-dep Mutex<state>+Condvar counting semaphore. (2) set_rate(Option<u64>) on RateLimiter + BlockingRateLimiter: moved rate/capacity into the bucket behind the existing Mutex for live retune; None/Some(0) empties->unlimited no-op, Some(r>0) installs/replaces priming a full burst; byte-for-byte identical when set_rate is never called. (3) AdaptiveController: PURE/deterministic (injected MonoTime clock + injected cpu/rss/p95; NO Instant::now / NO resources-sampler calls inside) -> record_op(OpSample{bytes,latency,result:Ok|Throttle|HardErr}) accumulates goodput/rtt/rtt_min EWMAs; tick(now,cpu,rss,p95)->Decision{limit,target_rate} implements slow-start (x1.5 while goodput rises) -> AIMD (additive+1 up / x0.5 down), latency-gradient g=rtt_min/rtt hold/decrease, throttle/HardErr x0.5 + 15s cooldown, CPU>85 no-increase/>95 decrease, memory hard cap limit*p95<=fraction*total_ram, ceiling, ~15s keep-or-revert re-probe, target_rate=fraction*goodput_knee, hysteresis for convergence. PM VERIFY independent: lane fence = crates/snapdir-stores/{lib.rs,transfer.rs,adaptive.rs} ONLY (zero new deps, no Cargo.lock change); SHA lock OK (frozen format untouched). verification_cmd `cargo test -p snapdir-stores --locked adaptive -- --nocapture` exit0 -> 12 adaptive tests pass (8 controller: healthy-ramp-then-cap, high-cpu-no-increase, converges, rising-latency-holds-without-error, target-rate-fraction, memory-budget-caps, throttle-backoff+cooldown + 4 gate: async/blocking acquire/set-limit/shrink-no-deadlock), "test result: ok". Teammate also: full crate 104 lib + 5 integ + 1 doctest green, clippy -D warnings + fmt clean. pass_criteria and:[exit_code:0, regex_match "test result: ok"] BOTH -> PASS. WIRING IS THE NEXT GATE (adaptive-wire) — controller not yet plugged into real transfers. SPLIT COMMIT: code (stores, gatesmith-free) + ledger separate. NOT pushed. Phase 18: 2/7. (4) Adaptive readout: `jobs <in>/<ceiling> (auto <fraction>)` from the TRUE --adaptive politeness fraction threaded CLI->ProgressReporter::start->RenderMetrics.adaptive_fraction (NOT the meaningless current_limit/target_rate ratio); `(auto)` fallback when fraction unknown; plain `<in>/<jobs>` when not adaptive. 2026-06-06T00-22-35Z event=ESCALATION gate=checksum-cli-select owner=PM note=cli teammate (agent af9e9da98b129a448) delivered the IN-LANE work GREEN: global --checksum<blake3|md5|sha256> + SNAPDIR_CHECKSUM env + --checksum-bin alias + precedence resolver + CLI-local enum SelectedHasher (impl snapdir_core::Hasher) threaded through walk + snapshot_id; manifest/id fully select sha256(64-hex)/md5(32-hex) + a distinct selected id; BLAKE3 DEFAULT id BYTE-IDENTICAL on committed-dev vs patched binary (pinned-constant test). `cargo test -p snapdir-cli --locked checksum` 7/7 ok; full cli suite + clippy green; lane fence = crates/snapdir-cli/ ONLY (17 files, 16 of them trycmd help snapshots). NOT COMMITTED (held pending operator scope decision). BLOCKER — PLAN AUDIT WAS WRONG: the plan claimed "store sharding is digest-length-agnostic -> NO store change". FALSE. snapdir-stores content-addresses every object by **blake3(bytes)** and the manifest per-file checksum IS that object key (file_store.rs put_object L478 / get_object|persist verify L464 / snapshot-id recompute L312; same hardcoded Blake3Hasher::new() in fetch.rs, push.rs, sync.rs, s3_store.rs, gcs_store.rs, b2_store.rs = 7 files). Under --checksum sha256/md5, push/stage FAIL "integrity check failed: expected <sha256> got <blake3>" — proven via a stores-API repro AND the real binary. So a sha256/md5 snapshot canNOT round-trip through ANY store without a snapdir-stores lane change — which directly contradicts the operator-approved e2e acceptance ("SNAPDIR_CHECKSUM=sha256 snapdir push ... then fetch/pull round-trips and verifies") AND the cli gate's own clause "content-addressed object keys all use the selected algorithm". manifest/id (no store) work fully today. DECISION REQUIRED (AskUserQuestion): (A,recommended) add a checksum-stores-select gate (stores lane) threading the selected hasher through the store object-address/integrity/id across file/s3/gcs/b2 + push/fetch/sync (objects filed under their sha256/md5 address; NO frozen-source mutation — object_path/store.rs not SHA-locked, manifest FORMAT unchanged, only the checksum VALUE's algorithm); cli round-trip tests depend on it -> a sha256 snapshot is genuinely sha256 end-to-end (matches the consortium intent). (B) re-scope checksum-cli-select to manifest/id-only selection (produce a sha256 manifest for inspection/interop; push/fetch stay blake3) — cheaper but does NOT let users SHARE sha256 artifacts via a store (defeats the stated use case). (C) decouple object-address from the manifest checksum (store always blake3, manifest shows selected) — FROZEN-FORMAT semantic change + re-lock, not recommended. Tick exits on escalation; teammate work persists uncommitted.

Operator decisions (AskUserQuestion 2026-06-08): FULL scope (backoff + HONOUR server Retry-After + wire adaptive); conservative default 5 attempts/250ms base/x2/30s cap/full-jitter; expose retry-policy + a NEW request-rate (req/s) limiter + the byte cap, all on the --checksum precedence model (flag>env>PER-BACKEND default>global) with researched per-backend defaults overriding global. Gates: (1) retry-backoff-core (stores) — retry.rs: RetryPolicy + full-jitter backoff honouring Retry-After, hand-rolled SplitMix64 jitter (NO new dep), injectable Sleeper/Jitter, SDK-agnostic Attempt{err,transient,retry_after} engine. (2) backend-rate-limits (stores) — limits.rs for_scheme() table (s3 5500/3500 rps bytes-inf; gs 5000/1000 rps bytes-inf; b2 20/50 rps 25MB/100MB; file/external inf), cited to AWS/GCS/Backblaze docs + a req/s limiter by REUSE of the existing token bucket (acquire(1)/call; new TransferConfig.max_requests_per_sec). (3) stores-backoff-wire (stores, dep 1+2) — wrap key_exists/get_bytes/put_bytes through retry_async, extract Retry-After at the concrete SDK boundary, DISABLE each SDK's built-in retries so snapdir is the single authority. (4) adaptive-wire-live (stores, dep 2) — run_adaptive in fetch/push fed by classify_error(); ON by default for network stores, file:// stays run_concurrent. (5) ratelimit-cli-select (cli, dep 1+2) — --max-retries/--retry-base-ms/--retry-max-ms/--max-rate/--max-requests/--adaptive + SNAPDIR_* via manual-env resolvers (mirror resolve_checksum_algo), resolve_rate_limits(scheme) layering user>for_scheme>global. (6) ratelimit-docs (docs, dep 5). (7) phase21-complete (generic, human-checkpoint, dep all-6). VALIDATION: ruby/YAML parse OK, 148 gates total (7 new), no dup ids, all deps resolve. 2026-06-10T03:00:00Z gate=ssh-accel owner=stores verdict=pass git=3aa3989 evidence=.gatesmith/evidence/ssh-accel-2026-06-09T23-18-00Z.log note=HEADLINE GATE. Runtime-negotiated SNAPPACK accel in emitted ssh:// scripts: ONE combined probe RT (manifest + version --capabilities); dispatch NO_ACCEL -> dumb / caps-ok (exact ' wire=1 ' literal const-pinned to snapdir_stores::WIRE_VERSION) -> accel / FORCE_ACCEL-no-caps -> error naming host+wire+caps+probe-output+remedies. accel_push = objects-needed diff + send-pack|receive-pack --require-manifest (manifest-last in pack, remote commits after verified end trailer; empty diff still streams manifest-only pack = interrupted-push completion). accel_fetch = caps-only probe, needed-list (or SENDALL full list, both baked, runtime pick) -> remote send-pack | local receive-pack (untrusted stream re-verified locally). Fallback: probe/diff fail -> dumb; STREAM fail -> nonzero + incremental-retry hint (no silent fallback). ACCEL-ORACLE PROVEN: dumb vs accel pushes byte-identical (id + .objects set + blob bytes + manifest bytes). 10/10 accel tests ZERO skips (engagement asserted via logging remote wrapper); crate suite 76/76; fmt/clippy clean. verification exit 0; files_exist ok.
2026-06-10T03:30:00Z gate=loopback-sshd-suite owner=tests verdict=fail git=- evidence=.gatesmith/evidence/loopback-sshd-suite-2026-06-09T23-42-00Z.log note=PM re-run 3/7 passed, 4 FAILED — parallelism race in the new suite: tests share ONE pinned TMPDIR (/tmp/sd-cm-<pid>/) and run concurrently, so (a) staging TempDirs vanish mid-push ('tar: could not chdir', 'staged manifest not found'), (b) the no-leak assertion scans the SHARED dir and counts other in-flight tests' staging/cache dirs as leaks. Teammate's local run was green (ordering luck). FIX NEXT TICK: per-test TMPDIR isolation (each test its own pinned tmp subdir) + leak assertion scoped to the test's OWN tmp + never share staging dirs across parallel tests. Code diff left UNCOMMITTED in tree for the retry teammate.
2026-06-10T00:20:00Z gate=loopback-sshd-suite owner=tests verdict=pass git=afd5080 evidence=.gatesmith/evidence/loopback-sshd-suite-retry-2026-06-10T00-15-00Z.log note=RETRY PASS (failure_count was 1). Root cause of the fail: common::TempDir::new derived from env::temp_dir() which re-reads process-global TMPDIR at call time -> concurrent tests materialized dirs inside the leak test's pinned scratch and were wiped on its drop. Fix: fixed /tmp/sd-lb-<pid>-<tag>-<n> base (no env::temp_dir() reads remain, grep-verified), per-test TMPDIR pin inside the lock-covered EnvGuard, leak check scoped to the test's OWN scratch + filtered to script artifacts. Proven 3x default parallelism + 1x --test-threads=8 by teammate AND 3x by PM (7/7 each, zero skips); crate suite 83/0. Suite: dumb ssh+sftp roundtrips over real sshd, restricted-sftp-only, host-key-fail-closed + EXTRA_OPTS-cannot-weaken behavioral proof, accel oracle over sshd (engagement asserted via logging wrapper), fallback + FORCE_ACCEL designed error, idempotent re-push + scoped no-leak. verification exit 0; files_exist ok.

PROVED the two-layer safety model: (1) WRITE-TIME stat-guard catches the realistic spoof — a same-size A->B rewrite with mtime+atime forced back via utimensat STILL mismatches CopyGuard because ctime_ns advances on the write and utimensat cannot move ctime backwards -> persist re-hashes -> Integrity. ctime is the spoof-defeater. (2) READ-TIME backstop holds for a FULLY-DEFEATED guard — guard captured AFTER mutating to B so the StatGuarded skip is guaranteed to fire and clones B under checksum(A); both get_object(checksum_A) and fetch_files REJECT with Integrity/404, B never served as valid (the load-bearing proof). Plus: concurrent mid-stage mutation (8 schedules, joined racer thread) -> snapshot self-consistent; inode-reuse (ino mismatch -> re-hash); mtime-granularity collision; 36-combination seeded-by-index property loop asserting the global invariant (no object readable via get_object under a checksum its bytes do not hash to). 2026-06-15T06:52:00Z gate=dx-id-stdin-verify owner=adversary verdict=pass(VERDICT=REAL-BUG) git=ledger-only evidence=.gatesmith/evidence/dx-id-stdin-verdict.md note=INVESTIGATION verdict REAL-BUG. Root cause: `snapdir id` with NO PATH is documented (help-id + tests/cmd/help-id.trycmd: "omit to read a manifest from stdin") to read a manifest from STDIN, but the Command::Id handler (cli.rs:1051) calls build_manifest(path.as_deref(),..) with NO stdin branch -> resolve_root(None) (cli.rs:3394) falls back to std::env::current_dir() -> `id` with no arg silently WALKS CWD and ignores stdin entirely. So `manifest <dir> | id` hashes cwd, not the piped manifest; persona-2's "non-determinism" = cwd differing between runs (id<dir> itself IS deterministic; snapdir-core snapshot_id/manifest are CORRECT, unchanged). DECISIVE PROOF: manifest output byte-stable; b3sum(man)==id<dir>; `cd A; id < man`==`id A`; `cd B; id < man`==`id B`; `id < /dev/null`==`id < man` from same cwd => result is purely f(cwd), stdin irrelevant. Present in BOTH built 1.8.0 + installed 1.7.0. FIX LANE: cli stdin-read (implement no-PATH+piped-stdin => read manifest from stdin, strip #-lines, b3sum per snapshot_id incl trailing newline; no-PATH+TTY => loud error not silent cwd-walk; positional `id <dir>`/`id .` unchanged). Repro staged .gatesmith/pending-tests/dx_id_stdin.rs (assert_cmd, pins fixed-input/cwd-independence + manifest|id round-trip; FAILING by design). PM SCHEDULES fix triple: +dx-id-stdin-impl-cli (cli) +dx-id-stdin-review (adversary), dx-fix-verify dep rewired to dx-id-stdin-review. VERIFIED FINDINGS grounding the design (read-only investigation): (1) object path .objects/<h0:3>/<h3:6>/<h6:9>/<h9:> mechanically encodes the full 64-hex BLAKE3 (store.rs:79-118) -> checksum recoverable WITHOUT reading content; (2) precedent CopyTrust::TrustedObject already skips re-hash for content-addressed objects (file_store.rs:100-112); (3) CORRECTION to the operator premise — walk records the SYMLINK's OWN lstat mode+size, NOT the target's (walk.rs:562-567,614-619): checksum is read through the link (correct) but size=lstat(symlink).len() and mode=symlink mode, so a naive linked re-snapshot does NOT reproduce the original id (size+mode differ) -> the fast path is CHECKSUM-ONLY; faithful round-trip needs the source manifest (documented limitation); (4) object store ALWAYS addresses by plain BLAKE3 (file_store.rs:42,264) — a non-default --checksum-bin (md5/sha256) or keyed SNAPDIR_MANIFEST_CONTEXT makes the embedded hash the WRONG algorithm -> fast path MUST disable + re-hash; (5) trusting an address bypasses the verify-on-read discipline (clone_skip.rs:53-67) -> SNAPDIR_VERIFY_COPIES=1 forces re-hash (existing strict override). KEYSTONE locked: id byte-identical with/without the fast path on a healthy store; frozen manifest format UNTOUCHED (compute-path only, additive opt-in 'object-store roots' hint defaulting to none so cli call sites are unchanged). Plan updated (~/.claude/plans/cosmic-knitting-tide.md).
